tyle$}}{\makebox[6.64168pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.00845pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.86319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{e}}}_{{{b)}{d}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.79411pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.79411pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.28157pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.62968pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{k^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.64168pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.64168pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.00845pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.86319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{e}}}_{{{b)}{d}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.79411pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.79411pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.28157pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.62968pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{k^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.64168pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.64168pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.00845pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.86319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{e}}}_{{{b)}{d}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.79411pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.79411pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.28157pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.62968pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{k^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.64168pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.64168pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.00845pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.86319pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{e}}}_{{{b)}{d}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.79411pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.79411pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.28157pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.62968pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2F(t,t^{\prime})^{2}\delta^{(3)}_{ab}.

(53)

Combining these results yields

H_{ab}=\delta^{(3)}_{ab}\left[\frac{\partial F}{\partial t}+2F^{2}\right]-3u_{a}u_{b}\frac{\partial F}{\partial t}.

(54)

Repeating the calculation at
x^{\prime}
and applying the parallel transporters produces

\displaystyle\frac{\partial F}{\partial t}+\frac{\partial F}{\partial t^{\prime}}+F^{2}

\displaystyle=-8\pi P.

(55)

\displaystyle 3F^{2}

\displaystyle=8\pi\rho,

(56)

Importantly, note that the second relation is algebraic.

To solve this system, it is convenient to introduce

\delta t=t+t^{\prime},\qquad\Delta t=t-t^{\prime},

(57)

and define

F(t,t^{\prime})=\tilde{F}(\Delta t,\delta t).

(58)

Introducing the algebraic equation into Eq. (
56
), it becomes

\frac{\partial\tilde{F}}{\partial\delta t}=-\frac{8\pi}{3}(\rho+3P),

(59)

which is subject to

3\tilde{F}^{2}=8\pi\rho.

(60)

To proceed, we must specify an equation of state. Our proposal is to adopt the simplest alternative, directly analogous to what is done in standard cosmology: assume that the bitensorial pressure is proportional to the bitensorial density. It is important to emphasize, however, that the physical interpretation of these bitensorial quantities remains unclear. Furthermore, they represent only a small correction to the total density and pressure, which continue to be dominated by their classical components. Hence, they are not restricted to be nonnegative.

Assuming an equation of state of the form
P=\omega\rho
, with
\omega
a real constant, and using Eq. (
60
), Eq. (
59
) becomes

\frac{\partial\rho}{\partial\delta t}=-\sqrt{\frac{32\pi}{3}}\,(1+3\omega)\,\rho^{3/2}.

(61)

For concreteness, we restrict from this point onwards to Hadamard states. In this case, in the coincidence limit
[
64
]
,
\rho
goes as
(\Delta t)^{-4}
. However, from (
61
), one can show that, for
\rho
to diverge in the coincidence limit, it is necessary that
\omega=-1/3
. This, in turn, implies that
\rho
depends only on
\Delta t
. Consequently,

\mathchoice{k^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.42805pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.42805pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.81349pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.86676pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{abc}}}}{k^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.42805pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.42805pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.81349pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.86676pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{abc}}}}{k^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.42805pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.42805pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.81349pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.86676pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{abc}}}}{k^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[11.42805pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[11.42805pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.81349pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.86676pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{abc}}}}=\frac{2\tau}{(t-t^{\prime})^{2}}\,u_{[a}\,\delta^{(3)}_{c]b}.

(62)

where
\tau
is a constant with units of time. Observe that this expression is symmetric under
t\leftrightarrow t^{\prime}
.

At this point the model is formally solved: for any given homogeneous and isotropic matter content in a Hadamard state and any FRW background metric, the bitensorial connection satisfying the field equations is completely determined. The remaining task is to extract the physical information encoded in this object.

4.1
Effective geometrical description

From a mathematical standpoint, the model has been solved under the relevant assumptions. However, its physical implications remain to be extracted. In quantum theories, when certain degrees of freedom are ignored, one traces over them
[
65
]
. In this spirit, our proposal for obtaining local physical quantities at
x
is to treat the dependence on
x^{\prime}
effectively by integrating over it (restricted to a convex normal neighborhood).

The precise prescription we propose is as follows. Our goal is to extract an effective contorsion that is an ordinary tensor,
\mathchoice{\bar{k}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{c}}}_{{{a}{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1205pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.51463pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\bar{k}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{c}}}_{{{a}{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1205pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.51463pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\bar{k}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{c}}}_{{{a}{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1205pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.51463pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\bar{k}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{c}}}_{{{a}{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1205pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.51463pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}(x)
. Moreover, inspired by Bell’s theorem, the type of nonlocalities we aim to incorporate operate outside the light cone. Thus, for any point
x
we integrate over all
x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{C}
, where
\mathcal{C}
denotes the set of all points that are spatially separated from
x
and lie within a normal convex neighborhood. The integration takes the form

\mathchoice{\bar{k}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{c}}}_{{{a}{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1205pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.51463pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\bar{k}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{c}}}_{{{a}{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1205pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.51463pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\bar{k}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{c}}}_{{{a}{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1205pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.51463pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\bar{k}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{c}}}_{{{a}{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1205pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.51463pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}(x)=\frac{1}{V_{\mathcal{C}}}\int_{\mathcal{C}}\mathchoice{k^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{c}}}_{{{a}{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1205pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.51463pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{k^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{c}}}_{{{a}{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1205pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.51463pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{k^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{c}}}_{{{a}{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1205pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.51463pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{k^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{c}}}_{{{a}{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1205pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.51463pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}(x,x^{\prime})\sqrt{-g}\,\text{d}^{4}x^{\prime},

(63)

where
\sqrt{-g}\,\text{d}^{4}x^{\prime}
is the appropriate integration element and
V_{\mathcal{C}}
is the
4
-volume of
\mathcal{C}
, introduced for dimensional consistency. Note that this integral is well defined because
\mathchoice{k^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{c}}}_{{{a}{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1205pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.51463pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{k^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{c}}}_{{{a}{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1205pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.51463pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{k^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{c}}}_{{{a}{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1205pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.51463pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{k^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{c}}}_{{{a}{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1205pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.51463pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}(x,x^{\prime})
is a scalar with respect to
x^{\prime}
.

Moreover, under the symmetries at hand and within the level of approximation we are working, Eq. (
63
) reduces to integrating
\tau/(t-t^{\prime})^{2}
. In spherical coordinates, this becomes

\int_{\mathcal{C}}\frac{\tau}{(t-t^{\prime})^{2}}\sqrt{-g}\,\text{d}^{4}x^{\prime}=4\pi\tau\int_{\mathcal{C}}\frac{r^{\prime 2}}{(t-t^{\prime})^{2}}\,\text{d}t^{\prime}\,\text{d}r^{\prime},

(64)

where the angular coordinates integrate trivially. Since the integration is performed outside the light cone, the integration limits are not independent. In fact, the light-cone condition restricts the limits of the
t^{\prime}
integral to the interval
(-r^{\prime},r^{\prime})
. Moreover, given that we also work within a normal convex neighborhood, we assume that
r^{\prime}
extends up to a cutoff,
r_{c}>0
. Integrating over this region and normalizing with the volume yields

\tau\frac{\int_{0}^{r_{c}}\int_{-r^{\prime}}^{r^{\prime}}\frac{r^{\prime 2}}{(t-t^{\prime})^{2}}\,\text{d}t^{\prime}\,\text{d}r^{\prime}}{\int_{0}^{r_{c}}\int_{-r^{\prime}}^{r^{\prime}}r^{\prime 2}\,\text{d}t^{\prime}\,\text{d}r^{\prime}}=\phi(t)

(65)

where we define

\phi(t)=-\frac{2\tau}{r_{c}^{2}}\left(\frac{1}{2}+\frac{t^{2}}{r_{c}^{2}}\ln\left[1-\frac{r_{c}^{2}}{t^{2}}\right]\right).

(66)

Thus, the effective contorsion becomes

\mathchoice{\bar{k}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1205pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.51463pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{a}{b}{c}}}}{\bar{k}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1205pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.51463pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{a}{b}{c}}}}{\bar{k}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1205pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.51463pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{a}{b}{c}}}}{\bar{k}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1205pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.51463pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{a}{b}{c}}}}(x)=2\phi(t)u_{[a}\,\delta^{(3)}_{c]b}.

(67)

We want to extract an effective cosmological constant. A natural approach is to compute the Ricci curvature associated with
\mathchoice{\bar{k}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{c}}}_{{{a}{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1205pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.51463pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\bar{k}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{c}}}_{{{a}{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1205pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.51463pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\bar{k}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{c}}}_{{{a}{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1205pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.51463pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\bar{k}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.33765pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.59009pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.85005pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.51666pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1029pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.50208pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{c}}}_{{{a}{b}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1205pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.51463pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}
, denoted
R_{ab}(\bar{k})
, and compare it with the Ricci tensor of the FRW metric,
R_{ab}(a)
. The only nonzero components are the time–time component and those with identical spatial indices. Comparing these components as obtained by the two methods yields

\displaystyle-3\frac{\ddot{a}}{a}

\displaystyle=-3\dot{\phi},

(68)

\displaystyle 2\phi^{2}+\dot{\phi}

\displaystyle=\ddot{a}a+2\dot{a}^{2}.

(69)

Since we are only interested in the late-time universe, we work within the approximation in which the time variation of
\phi
is negligible. Thus, we take
\phi(t)\approx\phi(t_{0})
and find

\phi(t_{0})^{2}=\dot{a}(t_{0})^{2},

(70)

or equivalently,

H_{0}=\frac{2}{r_{c}^{2}}\left|\frac{\tau}{2}+\frac{\tau t_{0}^{2}}{r_{c}^{2}}\ln\!\left(1-\frac{r_{c}^{2}}{t_{0}^{2}}\right)\right|.

(71)

Note that this expression represents a strictly positive additional contribution to the classical cosmological constant that turns on in the late universe. Hence, it may offer an explanation for the
H_{0}
tension
[
66
]
. For a complete assessment, however, the predictions of the model in the early universe must also be considered, a task we leave for future work.

Interestingly, Eq. (
71
) shows that the analysis forbids
r_{c}
from being equal to
t_{0}
. This suggests that
r_{c}
must lie below the Hubble radius. To obtain a simpler expression useful for constraining the free parameter, we consider the regime
r_{c}\ll t_{0}
. To leading order, Eq. (
71
) becomes

H_{0}=\frac{|\tau|}{r_{c}^{2}}.

(72)

Using the observed value
H_{0}\approx 10^{-18}\,\mathrm{s}^{-1}
, assuming that classical matter fields do not contribute to the cosmological constant, and taking into account the experimentally established fact
[
67
,
68
]
that quantum entanglement has been produced over distances
r_{c}>10^{6}\,\mathrm{m}
, we obtain the bound

|\tau|>10^{-6}\,\mathrm{s},

(73)

which is extremely small when compared with cosmological time scales. We now turn to the effects of the model in the Newtonian limit.

5
Newtonian limit

One of the most pressing questions in modern physics is: How does matter in a quantum state gravitate? An illustrative example is a source prepared in a spatial superposition. Analogous questions naturally arise for superpositions associated with other properties, such as energy or momentum. Fortunately, the bitensorial model proposed here provides a framework that can address these issues and extends beyond the scope of semiclassical gravity.

Throughout this analysis we assume that spacetime is nearly flat, so that the linearized Einstein equations hold. Hence, we work to first order in
\epsilon
, under the assumption that the bitensorial connection is itself of order
\epsilon
. In addition, the Newtonian limit requires a nonrelativistic approximation in which all relative velocities are much smaller than the speed of light. This can be effectively implemented by expanding around the formal limit in which the speed of light is taken to be infinite.

The first step in this analysis is to identify an appropriate bitensorial description of the matter fields. To address this point we can rely on insights from conventional Quantum Field Theory, as is done next.

5.1
Matter description

We begin this subsection by constructing the two-point function for a real, massive Klein–Gordon field, which we use to model matter. The smeared field operator can be written as

\phi(x)=\int\text{d}^{3}p\left(f^{*}(\vec{p})e^{-ip\cdot x}a^{\dagger}_{\vec{p}}+f(\vec{p})e^{ip\cdot x}a_{\vec{p}}\right),

(74)

where
f(\vec{p})
is the smearing function. Here the star denotes complex conjugation, and
p^{a}
is the on-shell four-momentum with spatial components
\vec{p}
. The operators
a^{\dagger}_{\vec{p}}
and
a_{\vec{p}}
are the creation and annihilation operators, respectively, associated with
\vec{p}
. We note that, in standard Quantum Field Theory, the form of
f(\vec{p})
is irrelevant since scattering amplitudes are extracted via the LSZ reduction formula
[
69
]
, which relies on asymptotic states. In contrast, the present work focuses on finite-time, localized quantum configurations, where the choice of smearing function can play a nontrivial role.

The corresponding two-point function is

G(x,x^{\prime})=\bra{0}\phi(x)\phi(x^{\prime})\ket{0}=\chi(x)\chi^{\dagger}(x^{\prime})+\chi(x^{\prime})\chi^{\dagger}(x)+H(x,x^{\prime}),

(75)

where
H(x,x^{\prime})
denotes the divergent contribution, which is typically removed by normal ordering, and

\chi(x)=\int\text{d}^{3}p\,f(\vec{p})\,e^{-ip\cdot x}.

(76)

For the purposes of the present calculation, we take
f(\vec{p})
to be real, purely for convenience, and we neglect the term
H(x,x^{\prime})
, as is commonly done. It is worth recalling, however, that
H(x,x^{\prime})
does play a role, as it effectively encodes a boundary condition.

The energy–momentum bitensor, given by the point-splitting prescription, is

\braket{T_{ab^{\prime}}}=\partial_{a}\partial_{b^{\prime}}G(x,x^{\prime})-\frac{1}{2}\eta_{ab^{\prime}}\left(\mathchoice{g^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.42123pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.42123pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.583pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.97713pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{c}}}_{{{c^{\prime}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1205pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.51463pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{g^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.42123pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.42123pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.583pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.97713pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{c}}}_{{{c^{\prime}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1205pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.51463pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{g^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.42123pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.42123pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.583pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.97713pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{c}}}_{{{c^{\prime}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1205pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.51463pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{g^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.42123pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.42123pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.583pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.97713pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{c}}}_{{{c^{\prime}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1205pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.51463pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\partial_{c}\partial^{c^{\prime}}+m^{2}\right)G(x,x^{\prime}).

(77)

This expression follows from the classical energy–momentum tensor, with the squared field replaced by
G(x,x^{\prime})
. Note that, given the bitensorial structure, each derivative acts on a quantity that behaves as a scalar.

For concreteness, we adopt a Gaussian smearing function
f(\vec{p})
, which yields Gaussian spatial distributions. We also focus on a static configuration in which the particle acting as the gravitational source is in a superposition of being centered around the spatial points
(0,0,a)
, with amplitude
\alpha
, and
(0,0,-a)
, with amplitude
\beta=\sqrt{1-\alpha^{2}}
(again, the amplitudes are taken to be real, for simplicity).

Clearly, the setup exhibits cylindrical symmetry around the
z
-axis. Thus, it is convenient to introduce the distance to this axis,
\rho
, which satisfies
\rho^{2}=x^{2}+y^{2}
(not to be confused with the density introduced in Sec.
4
). Under these assumptions we can write

\chi(\vec{x})=\frac{\chi_{z}(z)}{(m\sigma^{3})^{1/2}(2\pi)^{3/2}}\exp\!\left(-\frac{1}{2}\frac{\rho^{2}}{\sigma_{\perp}^{2}}\right),

(78)

where

\chi_{z}(z)=\alpha\,\exp\!\left[-\frac{(z+a)^{2}}{2\sigma_{z}^{2}}\right]+\beta\,\exp\!\left[-\frac{(z-a)^{2}}{2\sigma_{z}^{2}}\right].

(79)

Here
\sigma_{x}=\sigma_{\perp}=\sigma_{y}
and
\sigma^{3}=\sigma_{\perp}^{2}\sigma_{z}
denotes the product of the Gaussian widths.

In the Newtonian limit, the dominant component of the energy–momentum tensor is the time–time component. In the static case this component is further dominated by

\braket{T_{00^{\prime}}}\approx\frac{m^{2}}{2}\,G(x,x^{\prime}).

(80)

This is the gravitational source we use to study Newtonian limit of the model.

5.2
Bitensorial Einstein equation

Since the matter source is static, the geometrical side of Eq. (
46
) is also dominated by the time–time component, which takes the form

\delta^{b}_{0^{\prime}}\,\eta_{0b}\eta^{de}\left[\partial_{c}\mathchoice{k^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.79411pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.79411pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.28157pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.62968pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{c}}}_{{{d}{e}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1205pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.51463pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{k^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.79411pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.79411pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.28157pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.62968pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{c}}}_{{{d}{e}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1205pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.51463pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{k^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.79411pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.79411pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.28157pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.62968pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{c}}}_{{{d}{e}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.57375pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.1205pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.51463pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{k^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.16287pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.55038pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.8217pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.79411pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.79411pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.28 \textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.74406pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.09218pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{c^{\prime}}}}_{{{d^{\prime}}{e^{\prime}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.42123pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.42123pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.583pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.97713pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{k^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.01288pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.2842pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.6416pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.6416pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.74406pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.09218pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{c^{\prime}}}}_{{{d^{\prime}}{e^{\prime}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.42123pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.42123pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.583pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.97713pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}-\partial_{e^{\prime}}\mathchoice{k^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.01288pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.2842pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.42123pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.42123pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.583pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.97713pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{c^{\prime}}}}_{{{d^{\prime}}{c^{\prime}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.42123pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.42123pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.583pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.97713pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{k^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.01288pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.2842pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.42123pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.42123pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.583pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.97713pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{c^{\prime}}}}_{{{d^{\prime}}{c^{\prime}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.42123pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.42123pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.583pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.97713pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{k^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.01288pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.2842pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.42123pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.42123pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.583pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.97713pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{c^{\prime}}}}_{{{d^{\prime}}{c^{\prime}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.42123pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.42123pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.583pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.97713pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{k^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[6.01036pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[4.01288pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[3.2842pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.42123pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.42123pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.583pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.97713pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{c^{\prime}}}}_{{{d^{\prime}}{c^{\prime}}\mathchoice{\makebox[5.42123pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.42123pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.583pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.97713pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\right].

(81)

Using the index symmetries of the contorsion, the spatial components can be conveniently written as

\mathchoice{k^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}{j}{k}}}}{k^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}{j}{k}}}}{k^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}{j}{k}}}}{k^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{{i}{j}{k}}}}(x,x^{\prime})=\mathchoice{\epsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\ell}}}_{{{i}{k}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.34726pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.34726pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.45834pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\epsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\ell}}}_{{{i}{k}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.34726pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.34726pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.45834pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\epsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\ell}}}_{{{i}{k}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.34726pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.34726pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.45834pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\epsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\ell}}}_{{{i}{k}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.34726pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.34726pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.45834pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\,v_{j\ell}(x,x^{\prime}),

(82)

where
\mathchoice{\epsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\ell}}}_{{{i}{k}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.34726pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.34726pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.45834pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\epsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\ell}}}_{{{i}{k}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.34726pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.34726pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.45834pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\epsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\ell}}}_{{{i}{k}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.34726pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.34726pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.45834pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\epsilon^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\ell}}}_{{{i}{k}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.34726pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.34726pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.45834pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}
is the volume element of three-dimensional Euclidean space, and the indices are raised with the Euclidean metric
\delta_{j\ell}=\mathrm{diag}(1,1,1)
. In this expression,
v_{j\ell}
is a bitensor of type
(0,2)\times(0,0)
, and its antisymmetric part, which is the only component that contributes to the present calculation, is denoted by
v^{A}_{j\ell}
.

It is now straightforward to find the relevant bitensorial equation. A direct computation shows that substituting the ansatz (
82
) into Eq. (
81
), and equating it to Eq. (
80
), yields

\displaystyle\epsilon^{kjl}\partial_{k}v_{jl}^{A}+\epsilon^{k^{\prime}j^{\prime}l^{\prime}}\partial_{k^{\prime}}v^{A}_{j^{\prime}l^{\prime}}=4\pi m^{2}\,\chi(\vec{x})\chi(\vec{x}^{\prime}).

(83)

This equation may be rewritten as

\displaystyle\partial_{x}B+\partial_{y}C+\partial_{z}A+\partial_{x^{\prime}}B+\partial_{y^{\prime}}C+\partial_{z^{\prime}}A=2\pi m^{2}\chi(\vec{x})\chi(\vec{x}^{\prime}),

(84)

where the independent components of
v^{A}_{j\ell}
, in cylindrical symmetry, are defined as

v^{A}_{xy}=A(\vec{x},\vec{x}^{\prime}),\quad v^{A}_{yz}=B(\vec{x},\vec{x}^{\prime})=v^{A}_{zx}.

(85)

What is more, this symmetry allows us to write

\chi(\vec{x})=\frac{\chi_{z}(z)}{(m\sigma^{3})^{1/2}(2\pi)^{3/2}}\,\,\exp\!\left(-\frac{\rho^{2}}{2\sigma_{\perp}^{2}}\right).

(86)

At this stage it is important to recall that the model is underdetermined. This manifests itself here as having more unknowns than equations. To proceed, and to extract physical insight from this still incomplete yet valuable model, we impose
B=0
. Under these considerations, the equation to solve becomes

\displaystyle\partial_{z}A(\vec{x},\vec{x}^{\prime})+\partial_{z^{\prime}}A(\vec{x},\vec{x}^{\prime})=\frac{m\chi_{z}(z)\chi_{z}(z^{\prime})}{(2\pi)^{2}\sigma^{3}}\exp\!\left(-\frac{\rho^{2}+\rho^{\prime 2}}{2\sigma_{\perp}^{2}}\right).

(87)

The ansatz

A(\vec{x},\vec{x}^{\prime})=A_{1}(z)\,A_{2}(z^{\prime})\,A_{3}(\rho,\rho^{\prime}),

leads to

A_{3}(\rho,\rho^{\prime})\left[A_{2}(z^{\prime})\frac{\text{d}A_{1}(z)}{\text{d}z}+A_{1}(z)\frac{\text{d}A_{2}(z^{\prime})}{\text{d}z^{\prime}}\right]=\frac{m\,\chi_{z}(z)\chi_{z}(z^{\prime})}{(2\pi)^{2}\sigma^{3}}\exp\!\left(-\frac{\rho^{2}+\rho^{\prime 2}}{2\sigma_{\perp}^{2}}\right).

(88)

This expression allow us to conclude that

A_{3}(\rho,\rho^{\prime})=\frac{m}{(2\pi)^{2}\sigma^{3}}\exp\!\left(-\frac{\rho^{2}+\rho^{\prime 2}}{2\sigma_{\perp}^{2}}\right).

(89)

The remaining equation to solve is

A_{2}(z^{\prime})\frac{\text{d}A_{1}(z)}{\text{d}z}+A_{1}(z)\frac{\text{d}A_{2}(z^{\prime})}{\text{d}z^{\prime}}=\chi_{z}(z)\chi_{z}(z^{\prime}),

which can be rewritten in terms of
A_{4}(h)
, with
h=z+z^{\prime}
, as

\frac{\text{d}A_{4}(h)}{\text{d}h}=\chi_{z}(h-z^{\prime})\,\chi_{z}(h-z).

Integrating this expression yields

\displaystyle A_{4}(z,z^{\prime})=

\displaystyle\,\sigma_{z}\frac{\sqrt{\pi}}{2}\,\exp\!\left[-\frac{(z-z^{\prime})^{2}}{4\sigma_{z}^{2}}\right]\Bigg[\alpha^{2}\operatorname{Erf}\!\left(\frac{a+(z+z^{\prime})/2}{\sigma_{z}}\right)-\beta^{2}\operatorname{Erf}\!\left(\frac{a-(z+z^{\prime})/2}{\sigma_{z}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+2\alpha\beta\,\exp\!\left(-\frac{a^{2}}{\sigma_{z}^{2}}\right)\cosh\!\left(\frac{a(z-z^{\prime})}{\sigma_{z}^{2}}\right)\operatorname{Erf}\!\left(\frac{z+z^{\prime}}{2\sigma_{z}}\right)\Bigg],

where we use the “error function”

\operatorname{Erf}(z)=\frac{2}{\sqrt{\pi}}\int_{0}^{z}\exp\!\left(-t^{2}\right)\,\text{d}t.

(90)

Collecting all the terms, the solution of the model reads

\mathchoice{k^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{k}}}_{{{i}{j}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{k^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{k}}}_{{{i}{j}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{k^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{k}}}_{{{i}{j}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{k^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{k}}}_{{{i}{j}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}(x,x^{\prime})=A(\vec{x},\vec{x}^{\prime})\left[-\delta^{y}_{i}\delta^{y}_{j}\delta^{k}_{z}+\delta^{z}_{i}\delta^{y}_{j}\delta^{k}_{y}+\delta^{z}_{i}\delta^{x}_{j}\delta^{k}_{x}-\delta^{x}_{i}\delta^{x}_{j}\delta^{k}_{z}\right],

(91)

where

\displaystyle A(\vec{x},\vec{x}^{\prime})=

\displaystyle\,\frac{m\,\sigma_{z}\sqrt{\pi}}{2(2\pi)^{2}\sigma^{3}}\exp\!\left[-\frac{\rho^{2}+\rho^{\prime 2}}{2\sigma_{\perp}^{2}}\right]\exp\!\left[-\frac{(z-z^{\prime})^{2}}{4\sigma_{z}^{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\times\Bigg[\alpha^{2}\operatorname{Erf}\!\left(\frac{a+(z+z^{\prime})/2}{\sigma_{z}}\right)-\beta^{2}\operatorname{Erf}\!\left(\frac{a-(z+z^{\prime})/2}{\sigma_{z}}\right)

\displaystyle+2\alpha\beta\,\exp\!\left(-\frac{a^{2}}{\sigma_{z}^{2}}\right)\cosh\!\left(\frac{a(z-z^{\prime})}{\sigma_{z}^{2}}\right)\operatorname{Erf}\!\left(\frac{z+z^{\prime}}{2\sigma_{z}}\right)\Bigg].

(92)

Thus far, we have obtained the bitensorial connection that is a solution of the experimental configuration under consideration. In the next subsection, we construct the corresponding effective tensorial quantities.

5.3
Physical implications

As in the cosmological case, the effective connection is obtained by integrating the bitensorial connection, given in Eq. (
91
), over the primed coordinates. However, in the nonrelativistic setting there is no light cone: it collapses into the Newtonian instantaneous surface. Consequently, the effective connection is given by

\mathchoice{\bar{k}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{k}}}_{{{i}{j}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\bar{k}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{k}}}_{{{i}{j}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\bar{k}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{k}}}_{{{i}{j}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\bar{k}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{k}}}_{{{i}{j}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}(\vec{x})=\frac{1}{L^{3}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathchoice{k^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{k}}}_{{{i}{j}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{k^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{k}}}_{{{i}{j}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{k^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{k}}}_{{{i}{j}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{k^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{k}}}_{{{i}{j}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.42017pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.7052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.93228pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}(\vec{x},\vec{x}^{\prime})\,\text{d}^{3}x^{\prime},

(93)

where the relevant integration domain is
\mathbb{R}^{3}
, which is a normal convex neighborhood (a curvature cutoff would be of order
\epsilon
and is therefore negligible at the present level of approximation). The factor
L^{3}
, corresponding to the volume of the integration region, is included to ensure the correct units.

The relevant quantity to be integrated is
A(\vec{x},\vec{x}^{\prime})
. We define

\bar{A}(\vec{x})=\frac{1}{L^{3}}\int A(\vec{x},\vec{x}^{\prime})\,\mathrm{d}^{3}x^{\prime}=\bar{A}_{z}(z)\,\bar{A}_{\perp}(\rho),

(94)

where

\displaystyle\bar{A}_{z}(z)=

\displaystyle\,\frac{1}{L}\int\exp\!\left[-\frac{(z-z^{\prime})^{2}}{4\sigma_{z}^{2}}\right]\Bigg[\alpha^{2}\,\operatorname{Erf}\!\left(\frac{a+\frac{z+z^{\prime}}{2}}{\sigma_{z}}\right)-\beta^{2}\,\operatorname{Erf}\!\left(\frac{a-\frac{z+z^{\prime}}{2}}{\sigma_{z}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+2\alpha\beta\,\exp\!\left(-\frac{a^{2}}{\sigma_{z}^{2}}\right)\cosh\!\left(\frac{a(z-z^{\prime})}{\sigma_{z}^{2}}\right)\operatorname{Erf}\!\left(\frac{z+z^{\prime}}{2\sigma_{z}}\right)\Bigg]\,\mathrm{d}z^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\pi\sigma_{z}}{L}\!\left[\operatorname{Erf}\!\left(\frac{z+a}{\sqrt{2}\sigma_{z}}\right)(\alpha^{2}+\alpha\beta)+\operatorname{Erf}\!\left(\frac{z-a}{\sqrt{2}\sigma_{z}}\right)(\beta^{2}+\alpha\beta)\right].

(95)

Note that, in the last step, we use the identity

\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\operatorname{Erf}(z^{\prime})\,\exp\!\left[-(az^{\prime}+b)^{2}\right]\mathrm{d}z^{\prime}=-\frac{\sqrt{\pi}}{a}\,\operatorname{Erf}\!\left(\frac{b}{\sqrt{a^{2}+1}}\right).

(96)

Moreover, we have

\bar{A}_{\perp}(\rho)=\frac{m\sigma_{z}\sqrt{\pi}}{2L^{2}\sigma^{3}(2\pi)^{2}}\int\,\exp\!\left(-\frac{\rho^{2}+\rho^{\prime 2}}{2\sigma_{\perp}^{2}}\right)\rho^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}\rho^{\prime}\,\mathrm{d}\theta^{\prime}=\frac{m}{2\pi L^{2}}\,\exp\!\left(-\frac{\rho^{2}}{2\sigma_{\perp}^{2}}\right).

(97)

Combining these results yields

\bar{A}(\vec{x})=\frac{m\sigma_{z}}{2L^{3}}\left[\operatorname{Erf}\!\left(\frac{z+a}{\sqrt{2}\sigma_{z}}\right)(\alpha^{2}+\alpha\beta)+\operatorname{Erf}\!\left(\frac{z-a}{\sqrt{2}\sigma_{z}}\right)(\beta^{2}+\alpha\beta)\right]\exp\!\left(-\frac{\rho^{2}}{2\sigma_{\perp}^{2}}\right),

(98)

which, together with Eq. (
91
), gives the effective connection.

With this, we can find the force due to the quantum effects, which acts on top of that generated by the metric. We denote the components of this force by
F^{\mu}
, and define it through

u^{\nu}\mathring{\nabla}_{\nu}u^{\mu}=\frac{F^{\mu}}{M},

(99)

where
u^{a}
is the tangent of the trajectory of a test particle of mass
M
and
\mathring{\nabla}_{\nu}
is the derivative associated with the purely metric connection (to first order in
\epsilon
). We can readily show that

F^{\mu}=M\mathchoice{\bar{k}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}_{{{\nu}{\rho}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\bar{k}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}_{{{\nu}{\rho}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\bar{k}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}_{{{\nu}{\rho}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\bar{k}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.00928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.42052pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.72893pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.14899pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53337pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.80954pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{\mu}}}_{{{\nu}{\rho}\mathchoice{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.86232pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.95248pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10892pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}u^{\nu}u^{\rho}.

(100)

Thus, the temporal component of the effective force vanishes, as expected in the Newtonian limit, and the spatial components take the form

F^{i}=2M\bar{A}(\vec{x})\,v^{z}v^{i},

(101)

and

F^{z}=-M\bar{A}(\vec{x})v_{\perp}^{2},

(102)

where
i=x,y
,
\vec{v}
is the
3
-velocity of the test particle (which coincides with the initial
3
-velocity to first order in
\epsilon
), and
v_{\perp}^{2}=(v^{x})^{2}+(v^{y})^{2}
. Observe that the effective force is quadratic on the test particle’s velocity. This behavior is not entirely new: it also appears when considering gravitoelectromagnetic phenomena
[
70
]
. However, this is a clear indication that this effective force is not conservative.

Another notable feature is that the force components are proportional to the Gaussian width in the
z
direction,
\sigma_{z}
. Consequently, as
\sigma_{z}\rightarrow 0
, the effective force vanishes. In other words, in the most classical configuration of the source, the effective force disappears. In this limit, the function given in Eq. (
78
) reduces to a sum of Dirac deltas. Still, to consider a physically realistic configuration, one needs to assume that the particle is confined in a harmonic trap
[
71
]
, for which the dispersion satisfies

\sigma_{z}=\sqrt{\frac{\hbar}{m\omega_{z}}}.

(103)

Thus, when
\hbar\to 0
,
\sigma_{z}
vanishes, and so does the effective force.

Next, we address whether the effective force is attractive, as expected for a gravitational effect. The simplest way to verify this is to turn off one of the packets by setting, say,
\alpha=0
. For a test particle traveling in the
z=0
plane, when it is at the origin, it is straightforward to see that
F^{x}=0=F^{y}
. Thus, the effective force becomes

F^{z}=\frac{\sigma_{z}mMv_{\perp}^{2}}{2L^{3}}\,\operatorname{Erf}\!\left(\frac{a}{\sqrt{2}}\right),

(104)

which is positive, indicating that the force points toward the packet. This calculation can be generalized to the case where both packets are present. In this situation, the effective force on the test particle still behaves as an attractive one, with a factor
\alpha^{2}-\beta^{2}
determining the force direction.

An interesting limit is
\alpha=\beta
. This case is clearly invariant under
z\rightarrow-z
. Indeed, since the error function is odd, the effective force satisfies
\bar{A}(x,y,-z)=-\bar{A}(x,y,z)
. Also, the effective force vanishes at the origin for all particles traveling in the
z=0
plane, as expected.

In the far region,
z\gg a
,
F^{z}
becomes independent of
\alpha
and
\beta
, meaning that the test particle experiences the total gravitational influence of the superposed source. Finally, for
a\ll\sigma_{z}
and
z\ll\sigma_{z}
, a Taylor expansion of the error function yields

F^{z}=-\frac{\sigma_{z}mMv^{2}}{\sqrt{2\pi}L^{3}}\exp\!\left(-\frac{\rho^{2}}{2\sigma_{\perp}^{2}}\right)\left[\frac{z}{\sigma_{z}}+\frac{a}{\sigma_{z}}\left(\alpha^{2}-\beta^{2}\right)\right],

(105)

which, at least near
\rho=0
, resembles the force of a simple harmonic oscillator plus a constant shift toward the heavier packet. These properties demonstrate that the model reproduces all the expected symmetries of the setup, offering a nontrivial check of its internal consistency. In addition, the model predicts a definite effective gravitational force exerted by a particle in a spatial superposition on a test particle; a concrete effect that may, in principle, be accessible to experimental scrutiny.

6
Conclusions

In this work, inspired by Bell’s inequalities and by the structure of the expectation value of the energy–momentum tensor in Quantum Field Theory, we have proposed a bitensorial extension of GR. The bitensorial aspects of the model are incorporated directly into the connection, yielding a purely geometric yet intrinsically nonlocal theory. Notably, under the assumptions of homogeneity and isotropy, and in the Newtonian limit, the model becomes analytically solvable.

In the cosmological context, the late-time solution produces a positive contribution to the cosmological constant and reveals a relation between geometric quantities, such as the “size” of a normal convex neighborhood, and quantum correlations. The model is expected to be particularly relevant in high-curvature regimes, including the early universe. Once the early-universe analysis is completed, it may be possible to assess the model’s implications for current cosmological tensions, such as the one involving
H_{0}
.

We have also derived an effective force within the Newtonian limit. This force depends on the test particle’s velocity and is inherently nonconservative. Importantly, it is, in principle, amenable to empirical testing through precise measurements of deviations from standard Newtonian trajectories in laboratory-scale experiments.

Perhaps the central message of this work is that new and testable predictions can arise from “minimal” modifications to our existing theoretical framework, particularly when these modifications are guided by empirical insights such as the violation of Bell’s inequalities. In this way, the model brings GR and quantum mechanics closer together by incorporating features that have not typically been considered in gravitational physics. This example illustrates that it is not necessary to have a complete quantum theory of gravity to look for novel observable effects emerging from the interplay between gravitational and quantum phenomena. If any of the effects predicted by models of this kind were eventually observed, they would provide valuable guidance for future attempts to reconcile the two theories.

Finally, this framework offers a new perspective on the measurement problem. In standard semiclassical gravity, the collapse of a quantum state induces a discontinuity in the spacetime metric
[
41
,
42
]
. In contrast, within our bitensorial formulation, the collapse leads to a discontinuity in the bitensorial connection, whose physical implications remain to be explored.
